The Council murmured its assent. The intelligence officer closed her report. Outside the Merchant's Spire, the sun was setting over Verdantis, painting the city in shades of amber and gold. The caravans were still running. The markets were still open. The city was still alive. But the shadow of the Wengari legion was already falling across the desert, and nothing would ever be the same.





The Sunraptor banked low over the endless sand, its great wings beating with the tired, steady rhythm of a creature that had been flying for far too long. Cefiro sat in the saddle, his white fur dusty from the journey, his pale blue eyes scanning the horizon with the careful, deliberate attention of someone who was very good at not admitting when he was lost. Kira sat behind him, Pearl clutched against her chest, her dark eyes burning with the particular fury of a child who had been told a journey would take three days and was now on her fifth day in the air with no destination in sight.



<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"YOU SAID YOU KNEW THE WAY! IT'S BEEN FIVE DAYS! YOU SAID IT WOULD TAKE THREE!"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Cefiro did not turn around. His voice was still careful, still formal, but there was a defensive edge to it now. "I do know the way. I am merely... taking a scenic route."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"THERE'S NOTHING TO SEE! IT'S SAND! IT'S ALL SAND! THERE'S BEEN NOTHING BUT SAND FOR FIVE DAYS!"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The desert is very scenic. In its own way. If you appreciate subtle variations in dune formation and—"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Cefiro was quiet for a moment. When he spoke again, his voice was smaller. <span class="speech">"The stars were different. The constellations in the southern sky are not the same as the ones I used to navigate on the journey south. I thought I had compensated. I did not compensate enough. We have been flying slightly west of north for two days. We should have been flying directly north. I am sorry, cousin. I am lost."</span> The Sunraptor let out a long, rattling cry that sounded very much like agreement. Kira stared at the back of Cefiro's white head. <span class="speech">"You're lost. We've been lost for two days. And you didn't tell me."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I did not want to worry you."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I'M A BRIGHTMANE! WE DON'T WORRY! WE GET ANGRY! AND I AM VERY ANGRY!"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"That is... becoming apparent."</span> Cefiro banked the Sunraptor lower, scanning the sand below for any landmark. <span class="speech">"There. Do you see that? A caravan trail. Fresh. Perhaps a day old. If we follow it, we may find someone who can give us directions."</span> He paused. <span class="speech">"I will not tell them we are lost. I will tell them we are conducting a survey. Of the desert. For scientific purposes."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Kira pressed her face into Pearl's pale shell and screamed, very quietly, into the scorpion's carapace. Pearl's antennae waved in blunt sympathy. <span class="speech">"When we get home,"</span> Kira said, her voice muffled, <span class="speech">"I'm telling Ajani you got us lost for two days because you were looking at the wrong stars."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The stars were different. It is a legitimate astronomical error."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"You're a prince! Princes are supposed to know things!"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"I know many things. I know the names of all the stars in the northern sky. I know seventeen different ways to prepare snow hare. I know the complete lineage of the Snow Paw royal family going back twelve generations. I know—"</span> He stopped. <span class="speech">"I do not know how to navigate the southern desert. This has become apparent. I am sorry, Kira. Truly. I should have asked for a guide. I should have waited for a caravan. I should not have been so proud."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
The genuine regret in his voice cut through Kira's anger. She lifted her face from Pearl's shell. <span class="speech">"It's okay. Ajani got lost too. He was lost for months. At least you figured it out after two days."</span> She paused. <span class="speech">"Are we going to die out here?"</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"No. The Sunraptor can go several more days without rest. The caravan trail will lead us somewhere. And I still have the dried fish from the capital. It is not pleasant, but it is sustaining."</span> He paused again. <span class="speech">"Also, I would rather face your brother's anger than admit to my father that I got lost in a desert and let his favorite new ally's sister perish. My father is very large. And very disappointed. Constantly. It is his primary state of being."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira snorted. "Ajani's not that scary."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Ajani summoned six elemental spirits and killed a sixteen-meter Plague creature with a thunderstorm. He is terrifying. I am very afraid of him. I am also very afraid of my father. And Nadya. And, increasingly, you."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Good. You should be."</span> Kira settled back in the saddle, Pearl curled against her chest. <span class="speech">"Follow the caravan trail. And if we're not home in two more days, I'm flying this bird myself."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"You do not know how to fly a Sunraptor."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I'll figure it out. I figured out how to pickpocket in the market when I was five. How hard can a giant bird be?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Cefiro considered this. "That is a terrifying statement. I am choosing not to examine it further." He banked the Sunraptor toward the caravan trail, and they flew north, two cousins who had been lost and found and lost again, following a faint line of footprints across the endless sand.</p>
</div>


The sixth morning dawned hot and still over the desert. The Sunraptor rested in the shade of a rocky outcropping, its great wings folded, its head tucked beneath one feathered limb. Cefiro sat with his back against the stone, his notebook open in his lap, his pale blue eyes watching his young cousin with quiet curiosity.


Kira had drawn a line in the sand with her foot. She stood at the center of her makeshift arena, the practice saber Ajani had given her gripped in both paws, her dark fur already damp with sweat. The sun was barely above the horizon, and she had been drilling for half an hour. First form. Second form. Third form. The blade moved in steady, deliberate arcs, the green fire of Ajani's teaching absent but the precision of his discipline already taking root.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Cefiro closed his notebook. "Why do you practice every morning?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira did not stop moving. "Ajani said the saber is the survivor's weapon. He said if I learned nothing else, I should learn the saber. So I'm learning the saber." She completed the fourth form and moved into the fifth, her small body flowing through the stances with the careful, focused intensity of someone who had been taught by a king.</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"That is what he told you. I am asking why you do it. Every morning. Even here, in the desert, when no one is watching. When you are tired. When you are lost. When you could be sleeping. Why?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Kira finished the fifth form and lowered the practice saber. Her chest was heaving, her dark eyes distant. When she spoke, her voice was quieter than Cefiro had ever heard it. <span class="speech">"When the wall broke. The west wall. Where I lived. Where my parents died. I was six. I hid in the rubble for two days. I couldn't fight. I couldn't help. I couldn't do anything. I just hid."</span> She looked down at the saber in her paws. <span class="speech">"When the city was attacked again—the Plague War—I was in the royal chamber. Ajani was in a coma. The scorpions were with me. But I couldn't fight. I couldn't help. I just waited. Again."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Cefiro was silent for a long moment. Then he closed his notebook and rose to his feet. "The fifth form. Your left foot is dragging on the pivot. It slows you. If you shift your weight earlier, the blade will move faster. Show me again."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira stared at him. "You know the saber?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I know the ice blade. The Snow Paw variant. The principles are the same. Show me the fifth form again. I will correct your footwork."</p>
</div>


Kira raised the saber, and Cefiro stepped into the sand, his white fur catching the morning light, his pale blue eyes focused on her stance. The two cousins—one dark, one pale, both far from home—stood in the desert as the sun climbed higher, and the practice saber moved through its forms again.



Cefiro extended both paws, and the desert air around him crystallized. Two blades of ice formed in his grip—one short, barely the length of his forearm, its edge jagged and quick; the other long, nearly the length of a full saber, its surface smooth and cold and gleaming in the morning sun. They did not melt in the desert heat. They held their form, cold mist curling from their edges, the moisture in the air freezing into tiny crystals that swirled around his white paws.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"In the ice, one must always be ready for everything."</span> His voice was different now—still formal, still careful, but carrying the cadence of something learned long ago, something passed down through generations of Snow Paw warriors. <span class="speech">"The snow is treacherous. It hides crevasses. It buries landmarks. It shifts beneath your feet without warning. The wind plays tricks with the mind—it howls like a living thing, it steals your heat, it makes you see shapes that are not there. A warrior of the Ice City must be hard as the ice and quick as the wind. Hard enough to endure. Quick enough to adapt. Come at me, cousin."</span>
</div>


Kira raised her practice saber and charged. She was fast—Ajani had trained her well, and the months of morning drills had honed her reflexes—but Cefiro was faster. The short blade trapped her wooden saber mid-swing, the ice catching the wood and holding it, while the long blade came to rest gently against the side of her neck.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"You're dead, cousin. Again."</p>
</div>


Kira pulled back and attacked again. And again. And again. Each time, the short blade trapped her weapon. Each time, the long blade found her throat or her heart or her spine. Cefiro moved with an economy of motion that made Ajani's saber work look almost extravagant—each step was precise, each block was minimal, each counter was exactly where it needed to be and nowhere else.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Hard as ice. Quick as wind. You are being hard, but you are not being quick. You commit to each strike before you see my response. You must watch. You must wait. The ice does not attack the mountain. The ice waits for the thaw. Then it moves."</p>
</div>


Kira's legs buckled. She fell to her knees in the sand, her chest heaving, the practice saber still clutched in her paws. Pearl scuttled over and pressed her pale carapace against Kira's side, her antennae waving with concern.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Did you ever fight Ajani?" <span class="speech-attr">Kira asked, her voice hoarse.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Cefiro lowered the ice blades. The mist curled around his paws, and for a moment, something flickered in his pale blue eyes—a memory, distant and cold. <span class="speech">"Yes. Three years ago. When he came to the Ice City. He was half-frozen and half-starved, but he wanted to spar. He always wanted to spar."</span> He paused. <span class="speech">"It was more like a slaughter. I was faster. I was better trained. I had been fighting since I could walk. He was... stubborn. He kept getting up. Every time I put him down, he got up. In the end, he managed one hit. One. A glancing blow to my shoulder. He was very proud of it."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">He looked at Kira, still on her knees in the sand. "That was three years ago. I am not sure I could touch him now. The king I saw at the wall—the one who summoned six elemental spirits and killed a Plague creature with a thunderstorm—that is not the starving wanderer who stumbled into our city. He has become something else. Something more. I do not know if I could fight him now. I do not know if anyone could."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Kira pushed herself to her feet. Her legs were shaking, but her dark eyes were fierce. <span class="speech">"He's still the same person. He still laughs at his scorpions when they hide behind doors. He still visits the Tree even when he doesn't need to. He still wears the belt I made him, even though it's crooked."</span> She raised the practice saber. <span class="speech">"Teach me the ice blade. The short one. I want to learn."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Cefiro looked at her for a long moment. Then he extended his paw, and a second short blade of ice formed in the air beside him, its handle hovering at Kira's eye level. "Hard as ice. Quick as wind. Ready for everything. Begin."</p>
</div>


The Sunraptor banked over the frozen peaks, and Kira saw the Great Ice City for the first time.


It was not like Styxian. Styxian rose from the desert like a golden fist, its walls ancient and weathered, its hanging gardens a defiant burst of green against the endless sand. The Ice City rose from the tundra like a frozen wave, its spires carved from gleaming white stone that caught the pale northern sun and threw it back in a thousand fractured sparks. The walls were not walls but glaciers, shaped and smoothed by millennia of Snow Paw craft.

The gates were not bronze but ice, enchanted to never melt, their surface etched with the history of the family that had fled the Black Fire five thousand years ago and survived.


The Sunraptor descended through the freezing air, and Kira clutched Pearl against her chest, her dark fur bristling against the cold despite the heavy cloak Cefiro had draped over her. The city spread beneath them, and she could see figures moving in the streets—white-furred Wengari, tall and elegant, their pelts marked with black motes like Cefiro's, their breath misting in the frozen air. Children pointed upward at the Sunraptor. Adults paused in their work to watch it descend.


The landing platform was a wide circle of polished ice at the base of the palace. The Sunraptor touched down with a weary screech, its great wings folding, its head drooping with exhaustion. Cefiro dismounted first, his white fur blending with the snow, and extended a paw to help Kira down. Pearl skittered onto her shoulder, her pale carapace shivering slightly in the cold.


A delegation was already waiting. At its head stood a Wengari of absolutely massive proportions—Nikolai, Tsar of the Snow Paws, standing nearly two point eight meters from his paws to the tips of his ears. His white fur was thick and heavy, his black motes forming patterns like storm clouds across his shoulders. His pale blue eyes—the same eyes Cefiro had—fixed on his son with an expression of profound relief barely concealed behind a mask of royal formality.


Beside him stood Anastasia, the Tsarina, two and a half meters of lithe, beautiful white fur and a quiet, frightening demeanor. She did not speak. She did not need to. Her presence was a statement in itself—a blade hidden in silk, a winter storm waiting to break. She looked at her son, and the faintest softening at the corners of her mouth was the only sign that she had been worried.


Behind them stood two daughters. The older, Vanya, was twenty-seven years old and two point four meters tall, her pale blue eyes sharp and assessing, her posture that of a warrior who had been trained since birth to defend the Ice City. The younger, Nadya, was sixteen and already two point two meters, her white fur still soft with youth, her eyes blazing with the same fierce curiosity that had driven her to throw snowballs at a starving wanderer three years ago.


And standing slightly apart, trying very hard to look formal and failing utterly, was the youngest son. Ivan. He was the same age as Kira, the same height as Kira, and the moment he saw her—a dark-furred Wengari girl with a white scorpion on her shoulder, walking through the snow like she belonged there—his pale blue eyes went very wide. His mouth opened slightly. His tail gave a single, involuntary twitch.

Vanya noticed. Nadya noticed. Anastasia noticed. Nikolai noticed. No one said anything. Ivan did not stop staring.


<div class="dialogue-block">
Nikolai stepped forward, his voice deep and resonant, carrying the weight of five thousand years of isolation. <span class="speech">"Cefiro. You are home."</span> He looked at Kira, his pale blue eyes taking in her dark fur, her fierce expression, the practice saber still strapped to her back. <span class="speech">"And you have brought a guest. A Shadow Paw. The stories the wanderer told us were true. There are indeed more Wengari."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Cefiro inclined his head. "This is Kira Brightmane. Adopted sister of Ajani Brightmane, first of his name, White Dawn, king of the Wengari. She has come to see the Ice City and to bring word of the south."</p>
</div>


The silence that followed was absolute. Even the wind seemed to pause. Vanya's sharp eyes widened. Nadya's fierce expression flickered with something that was not quite shock and not quite delight. Anastasia's quiet, frightening stillness deepened. Nikolai's massive frame seemed to grow even larger. Ivan was still staring at Kira and had not heard a word anyone had said.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"He is king,"</span> Nikolai said. It was not a question. <span class="speech">"The starving boy who wandered into our city three years ago is now a king."</span> Cefiro nodded. <span class="speech">"He is not merely a king. He is a White Dawn. Convergence-born. Touched by both suns. He renewed the pact with the lord of the desert. He trained under the lord of the marsh. He freed the Pyrinae from three thousand years of vassalage. He held a tournament and put his crown on the line. He survived an assassination attempt, a coup, a coma, and a war. He summoned six elemental spirits and destroyed a Plague creature with a thunderstorm. He has united the four families under a new government. He has built offices and councils and trade routes. The Shadow Paws fight for him. The Motted Paws serve as his regent. The Stripe Paws carry his coin. The Bright Paws are learning to be strong again. And he sent me here with the royal seal and an invitation from one king to another."</span> He produced the seal from his cloak. <span class="speech">"Ajani Brightmane requests formal diplomatic relations with the Snow Paws. He wishes for the fifth family to come home."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Vanya broke the silence. <span class="speech"><span class="speech">"Is he married?"</span></span> Nadya shoved her sister. <span class="speech">"Vanya! He's a king! You can't just—"</span> <span class="speech">"I'm asking. It's a legitimate question."</span> She turned back to Cefiro. "Is he married?"
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Cefiro closed his eyes. "No. He is not married. He is, in fact, alarmingly single. Several individuals have attempted to rectify this. He has not noticed any of them."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nadya's eyes blazed. "I threw snowballs at him. For a month. He didn't notice either. I thought he was just being polite. I should have thrown more snowballs."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Anastasia spoke for the first time. Her voice was soft and cold, like snow falling on still water. <span class="speech">"The White Dawn. The Convergence-born. The one who was promised. We have kept the old tales. We have waited for this."</span> She looked at her son. <span class="speech">"You have done well, Cefiro."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Nikolai stepped forward and placed one massive paw on his son's shoulder. <span class="speech">"The journey was long. The news is longer. We will speak more inside."</span> He turned to Kira, and his stern face softened almost imperceptibly. <span class="speech">"Kira Brightmane. Shadow Paw. Sister of the White Dawn. You are welcome in the Ice City. Our home is yours."</span> Kira inclined her head. <span class="speech">"Thank you, Tsar Nikolai. Ajani sends his greetings. And he says to tell Nadya she still throws like a cub."</span>
</div>


Nadya's delighted laughter echoed across the frozen landing platform, and somewhere in the palace, a servant began preparing rooms for guests who had come from the other side of the world.


The dining hall of the Ice Palace was a vast chamber carved from living glacier, its walls enchanted to glow with a soft blue luminescence that cast no shadows. The table at its center was a single slab of ancient ice, polished to a mirror sheen, and the chairs were carved from the same frozen stone, their seats covered with thick furs of white bear and snow hare. The meal was a feast of northern delicacies—roasted ice fish, preserved snow berries, haunches of white bear meat, and a clear, potent liquor that the Snow Paws called Frostfire.

Kira sat between Cefiro and the empty chair that Ivan had claimed the moment he entered the room, his pale blue eyes fixed on her with an intensity that was almost alarming.


Ivan was trying very hard to get Kira to notice him. He had offered her the best cut of fish. He had refilled her cup of Frostfire before she had taken a single sip. He had asked if she was cold, if she was tired, if she needed more furs, if she wanted to see the northern lights from the observation spire, if she liked snow sculptures, if she had ever seen a white bear, if she—


Kira did not notice any of this. Her dark eyes were fixed on Anastasia, who sat at the far end of the table with the quiet, frightening stillness of a winter storm waiting to break. Pearl was on the table beside her plate, her pale carapace gleaming in the blue luminescence. The Tsarina was examining the scorpion with the careful, appreciative gaze of a master craftswoman.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"You're an ice smith,"</span> Kira said. It was not a question. She had been watching Anastasia since she entered the room, watching the way the Tsarina's paws moved, the way her pale blue eyes assessed everything she looked at. <span class="speech">"You enchant ice. You make things out of it."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Anastasia inclined her head. "I do. The walls of this city. The gates. The weapons our warriors carry. All of it is ice. All of it is enchanted. I have been the ice smith of the Snow Paws since before you were born."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Kira leaned forward. <span class="speech">"My mother was an armorsmith. She made armor for the Bright Paw guards at the west wall. She died when the Fire Beetles broke through. I was six."</span> She paused. <span class="speech">"I made a belt for Ajani. Fire scarab leather. I sewed it myself. The stitching is crooked on the left side because I ran out of thread, but he still wears it."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Anastasia's quiet stillness deepened. <span class="speech">"You are an armorsmith's daughter. You understand that what we do is not merely craft. It is protection. We wrap our loved ones in our work, and we hope it holds."</span> She reached out and touched Pearl's pale carapace. <span class="speech">"This scorpion. She is beautiful. She carries your affection for her in every movement. You have raised her well."</span>
</div>


Pearl's antennae waved with what could only be described as pride. She clicked her pincers once, and her tail curled in a graceful arc, as if presenting herself for inspection.


<div class="dialogue-block">
Kira smiled for the first time since she entered the city. <span class="speech">"Her name is Pearl. She deflected an assassin's blade. She's the bravest of the three."</span> She paused. <span class="speech">"Blackie and Reddy are bigger. They're with Ajani. They shoot dissolving beams from their tails now. I don't think Pearl can do that yet. But she's still the bravest."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Across the table, Ivan was listening with the rapt attention of someone who had never heard a story like this in his life. His pale blue eyes were wide with horror and wonder and something else entirely. <span class="speech">"An assassin,"</span> he breathed. <span class="speech">"Someone tried to kill you. In your own room."</span> Kira looked at him as if noticing him for the first time. <span class="speech">"It was a Bright Paw. He was part of the Black Dawn. They think all Convergence-born are catastrophes. Blackie melted him. I watched."</span>
</div>


Ivan's mouth opened. It closed. It opened again. No sound came out. Nikolai watched his youngest son with the quiet, knowing expression of a father who had already begun to hear wedding bells.


But Nikolai was also a king. A king who had just learned that his long-lost cousins were alive and thriving and led by a White Dawn who had accomplished more in a year than most monarchs did in a lifetime. A king who was itching to fight that White Dawn. He turned to Cefiro, his massive form dwarfing the chair he sat in.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Tell me about this war. The Plague War. You said the Humman Sultan deployed weapons from Velarius. You said there was a creature. You said Ajani summoned elemental spirits and destroyed it. Tell me everything. Numbers. Tactics. Casualties. The state of the Wengari military. The strength of their walls. The loyalty of their generals. Everything a king would need to know about a potential ally."</p>
</div>

<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Cefiro set down his cup of Frostfire. "The Humman army marched from Verdantis. Nine hundred kilometers. A thousand scorpion riders, fifty Tide Wolf cavalry, ten Amuk war-beasts."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"What is an Amuk?" <span class="speech-attr">Nikolai interrupted.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"A war-beast. Eight-legged. Five meters tall. Tusked. The Hummans use them as siege engines. They carry artillery on their backs."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai nodded slowly. "We have nothing like that in the north. Continue."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"They arrived at dawn. The capital was defended by a garrison of twenty elite soldiers and a hundred volunteers. The Woh riders reinforced at the first hour."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Woh riders. Theropods. Five meters tall. Natural plate armor. Gravity manipulation or AOE kinetic fields. The Stripe Paws breed them for war. They are terrifying. I saw them charge."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai's pale blue eyes narrowed. "We have nothing like that in the north either. The largest thing we ride is the Sunraptor, and they are not built for war."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The ghosts arrived at the second hour. Bats. Five-meter wingspans. Optical camouflage. The Motted Paws speak to them through drums."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Bats,"</span> Vanya said flatly. <span class="speech">"You are telling me the southern Wengari ride invisible bats into battle."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Not invisible. Camouflaged. And yes."</p>
</div>


Vanya looked at Nadya. Nadya looked at Vanya. Both of them seemed to be reconsidering everything they had imagined about the south.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The shadow riders arrived at the third hour. Black Tide Wolves. The southern variant. They carry the shadow flare—a remnant of the First Tyrant's fire. They can engulf themselves in it at will."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Nikolai's expression darkened. The First Tyrant. The Black Fire. The reason the Snow Paws had fled north five thousand years ago. The reason they had been alone in the ice for all those millennia. <span class="speech">"The Black Fire still burns,"</span> he said quietly. <span class="speech">"After all this time."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Not as it was. It is controlled now. The Shadow Paws train from cubhood to withstand it. They used it to break the Plague net."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Plague net."</span> Nikolai's voice was flat. <span class="speech">"You have not explained the Plague net."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Cefiro paused. He was telling the story in the wrong order. <span class="speech">"The Humman Sultan—Mekhmed—deployed weapons from the Fifth Tyrant. Suicide scorpions. Purple orbs. The orbs created a net of tendrils that trapped and consumed anything they touched. The shadow riders burned through it with the Black Fire. But the Plague weapons—"</span> He stopped. <span class="speech">"The Plague is a long story. It involves the Fifth Tyrant, Velarius Vane. He poisoned the world for sixty-five years. He died five hundred years ago. The Humman Sultan somehow acquired his old weapons."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai raised one massive paw. "The Fifth Tyrant. We do not know this name. We know the First—the Black Fire, who drove us north. We know there were others. The old tales speak of Convergence-born who rose and fell. But we have been isolated for five thousand years. We do not know the names of the later Tyrants. We do not know what they did. Explain."</p>
</div>


Cefiro looked at Kira. Kira looked at Cefiro. The Snow Paws had been alone in the ice for five thousand years. They had fled before the First Tyrant's genocide was complete. They had missed everything that came after.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"The Second Tyrant was Crr'zzak,"</span> Cefiro said. <span class="speech">"Chi'Thak. The Blight. He organized the swarm and devoured half the continent. The Third was Uthgar Lightbringer. Bright Paw. He made the pact with the lord of the desert and built the capital. He vanished three thousand years ago. The Fourth was Xal'thyra. Veylar. She silenced the male Styx and projected the Great Stillness across the world. The Fifth was Velarius Vane. Humman. He was a healer who became a monster. He created the Plague. It lasted sixty-five years. It killed eight percent of Ethra's population. His own weapons were used against the capital in this war. The creature that rose at the end—it was made from those weapons, and from something older. Something from the belt."</span>
</div>


The dining hall was silent. Even the Frostfire seemed to have stopped burning. Nikolai stared at his son. Anastasia's quiet, frightening stillness had deepened into something that was almost reverence—the stillness of a woman who was absorbing the weight of five thousand years of history in a single moment. Vanya and Nadya had stopped their whispered conversation. Ivan had forgotten to stare at Kira. He was staring at Cefiro instead, his pale blue eyes wide with the dawning realization that the world was much larger and much darker than he had ever imagined.



<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Five Tyrants,"</span> Nikolai said. <span class="speech">"Five. And the last of them was human. The weakest race on Ethra produced the worst monster in history. And his weapons were used against our cousins in a war that nearly destroyed them."</span> He paused. <span class="speech">"The White Dawn—Ajani—he destroyed this creature. With elemental spirits."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Yes. And a thunderstorm. And the help of every soldier, civilian, ghost, Woh, shadow rider, Pyrinae sorcerer, Golden Cloak, and volunteer who held the wall while he was unconscious."</p>
</div>




## Chapter 4: The Road Begins

<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">When Cefiro finished, the Tsar was silent for a long moment. Then he spoke, his deep voice quiet and contemplative. "I want to fight him. This White Dawn. This Ajani Brightmane. Three years ago, he was a starving boy who could not defeat my son in a sparring match. Now you tell me he is something else. Something more. I want to see it for myself."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"You will. The royal seal he gave me includes an invitation. He wants the Snow Paws to come south. He wants the fifth family to return. There will be a memorial celebration for the fallen. He wants us there."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Nikolai was silent for a long moment. Then he looked at his son, and his voice was very quiet. <span class="speech">"You have been in the south for less than a month. You have learned more about our people in that time than we have learned in five thousand years. The Snow Paws have been alone in the ice, tending our own fires, telling our own stories, and the world kept turning without us. Tyrants rose and fell. Pacts were made and broken. A Humman nearly destroyed the Wengari, and a White Dawn saved them. And we knew nothing of any of it."</span> He rose from his chair, his massive form casting a long shadow across the frozen table. <span class="speech">"Then we will go south. I will bring a delegation. I will bring my family. I will bring gifts. I will bring questions. And I will spar with this White Dawn. We will see if he is as strong as my son claims."</span>
</div>





<div class="dialogue-block">
Vanya and Nadya had been listening to their own conversation, their heads bent together, their pale blue eyes flicking toward Cefiro with the predatory focus of sisters who wanted information. Vanya spoke first, her voice sharp and direct. <span class="speech">"The women of the south. Are any of them strong? I mean truly strong. Warriors. Champions. I have been training my whole life. I want to know if there is anyone worth fighting."</span> Cefiro considered this. <span class="speech">"There is Sera. Shadow Paw. Master of the Eight Points. She held the gate against four hundred scorpion riders with twenty soldiers. Her face is ruined from the battle, but she did not break. There is Reva. Stripe Paw. Two-star general. She lost fifteen Wohs in three seconds and kept fighting. There is Yvaria. Motted Paw. Two-star general. She led the ghosts through the Plague mist and caught Ajani when he fell from the sky. There is Sylva. Motted Paw. Regent of the Wengari. She won the king's tournament. She is the most patient warrior I have ever seen. She defeated Ajani in single combat and then yielded so he could keep the crown. There is Lira. Shadow Paw. Master of the Eight Points. She guards the king's sister. She was presented as a bride and dismissed as a misunderstanding, but she is deadly. Any of them would be worth fighting."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Vanya's eyes blazed with anticipation. "Good. I will fight them all."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Nadya leaned forward. <span class="speech">"And the beautiful ones? The ones who are trying to marry Ajani? Are any of them as beautiful as me?"</span> She tossed her white mane over her shoulder with the practiced confidence of a sixteen-year-old princess who had never been told no. Cefiro closed his eyes. <span class="speech">"Yvaria has declared publicly that she will be his bride. She caught him when he fell from the sky. She has not left his side since the war. Lira was presented as a bride by the Shadow Paw elders. The king did not notice. The Pyrinae ambassador, a Sun-Walker named Mira, has been watching him with professional interest. The Motted Paw general, Yvaria—"</span> He stopped. <span class="speech">"I have already mentioned Yvaria. She is both strong and beautiful. She is also very persistent. Ajani has not noticed any of this. He is, as I have mentioned, alarmingly single."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nadya's eyes gleamed. "Then I still have a chance. I threw snowballs at him for a month. He didn't notice then, but I was twelve. I am sixteen now. He will notice."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"He will not notice,"</span> Cefiro said flatly. <span class="speech">"He did not notice the assassin who tried to kill him until the scorpion melted him. He is the least observant king in the history of the Wengari. I do not believe he would notice a mate if they climbed into his bed and announced their intentions in formal court language."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Anastasia was still examining Pearl. The scorpion had climbed into her lap at some point during the conversation, her pale carapace gleaming against the Tsarina's white fur. Pearl's antennae waved contentedly as Anastasia traced the lines of her chitin with one gentle claw. <span class="speech">"She is remarkable. I have never seen a creature like this. The southern deserts produce such strange and beautiful things."</span> She looked up at Kira. <span class="speech">"You said there are two more. Larger. With dissolving beams in their tails. Are they all this affectionate?"</span> Kira nodded. <span class="speech">"Blackie and Reddy are bigger. They hide behind doors and jump out at people. Ajani thinks it's funny. The servants don't. But they're good. They're protective. They love him. They love me. Pearl is the smallest, but she's the bravest. She's the reason I'm alive."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Anastasia looked at Pearl, who was now making a soft, rumbling sound that was almost a purr. <span class="speech">"I would like to meet the others. When we go south."</span> She looked at her husband. <span class="speech">"We are going south. I have decided."</span> Nikolai inclined his head. <span class="speech">"I had already decided. But I am glad we agree."</span>
</div>













<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">He looked at Kira, and his stern face softened. "Kira Brightmane. Sister of the White Dawn. You have traveled far and survived much. The Ice City is honored by your presence. Tomorrow, you will tell us more of your brother. Tonight, you will rest. Ivan, stop staring at the girl and escort her to her chambers."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ivan's face flushed a deep, mortified crimson beneath his white fur. "I wasn't—I wasn't staring. I was listening. There's a difference."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"There is no difference when your mouth is open," <span class="speech-attr">Vanya observed.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"It wasn't open!"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"It was a little open," <span class="speech-attr">Nadya added.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira looked at Ivan. Ivan looked at Kira. Pearl clicked her pincers once, and the sound managed to convey both amusement and sympathy in equal measure. Ivan rose from his chair with the stiff, formal posture of someone who had just been publicly humiliated by his entire family in front of the most interesting person he had ever met, and offered Kira his paw. "I will escort you to your chambers. The Ice City is easy to get lost in. There are many corridors. They all look the same. I know them all. I can show you."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira took his paw. "You're the same height as me."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I know. It's very convenient."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira looked at him for a long moment. Then, very faintly, the corner of her mouth twitched. "Fine. Show me the corridors." They walked out of the dining hall together, Pearl riding on Kira's shoulder with her antennae waving, and Ivan's tail gave a single, involuntary twitch of happiness.</p>
</div>



<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira's paw tightened around the wooden saber. Her dark eyes blazed with the same fierce intensity that had carried her through the streets of the capital, through the assassination attempt, through the war. "Take that back! No one can best Ajani!"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ivan drew his small ceremonial dagger—a blade of enchanted ice that glittered in the blue luminescence of the dining hall. "Of course they can! Brother bested him daily for six months! He told me so himself! The wanderer couldn't beat anyone when he was here!"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"That was three years ago! He's a White Dawn now! He summoned six elemental spirits! He killed a Plague creature with a thunderstorm! What has Cefiro done lately?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Cefiro is the best fighter in the Ice City! Everyone says so! Even Father says so! And Father is the best fighter in the world!"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Ajani is the best fighter in the world! He defeated Sylva of the Motted Paws! He held the gate against a thousand scorpions! He—"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Enough." <span class="speech-attr">Nikolai's deep voice boomed across the dining hall, cutting through the children's argument like a blade through snow. But when Kira and Ivan turned to face him, the Tsar of the Snow Paws was not stern. He was leaning back in his chair, his massive arms folded across his chest, his pale blue eyes gleaming with something that was unmistakably amusement.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"You truly are a Wengari southern princess. Every inch of you."</span> He looked at Ivan, who was still holding his ceremonial dagger with the white-knuckled grip of a cub who had just been challenged in front of his entire family. <span class="speech">"And you. My youngest son. Defending your brother's honor against a guest. There is only one way to settle this."</span> He rose from his chair, his massive form casting a long shadow across the frozen table. <span class="speech">"Why don't you spar in the arena? We will watch. It has been too long since we had a proper duel in the palace."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Vanya leaned forward, her sharp eyes gleaming with anticipation. "I want to see this. The southern princess against the ice prince. My money is on the small one."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Which small one?"</span> Nadya asked. <span class="speech">"They're both small."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The dark one. She has fire in her eyes. Ivan has a crush."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"It's not a crush!" <span class="speech-attr">Ivan's voice cracked on the word, which did not help his case at all.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira raised her wooden saber. "I accept. But when I win, you have to admit Ajani is the best fighter in the world."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Ivan raised his ice dagger. <span class="speech">"And when I win, you have to admit Cefiro is better. And also—"</span> He hesitated, his pale blue eyes flickering with something that was not quite courage and not quite fear. <span class="speech">"And also you have to let me show you the northern lights. From the observation spire. It's very beautiful. Everyone says so."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira stared at him. "Is this a duel or a courtship?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I don't know. I've never done either."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Anastasia watched the exchange with the quiet, frightening stillness of a mother who was already planning the seating arrangements for the wedding. Nikolai watched with the satisfaction of a father who had been hoping for exactly this outcome. <span class="speech">"The arena, then. We will go now. Ivan, fetch your proper sparring blade. Kira Brightmane, your wooden saber is acceptable. The duel will be until first blood or surrender."</span> He paused, and his voice dropped to a conspiratorial rumble that only Anastasia could hear. <span class="speech">"All of our children are single. It is not wrong for a father to dream."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Anastasia's quiet voice was barely a whisper. "It is not wrong. The Shadow Paw girl is fierce. She would be good for Ivan. He needs someone who will challenge him."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"They are six years old."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"In Wengari years, perhaps. In Snow Paw years, they are nearly adolescents. And in political years, a union between the southern crown and the northern throne would bind our families together for the first time in five thousand years. I am not merely dreaming. I am planning."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai looked at his wife with the deep, abiding admiration of a husband who had learned long ago that she was always several steps ahead of him. "You are terrifying."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I know. Let us go watch our son be defeated by a Shadow Paw princess. It will be good for his character."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">As they walked through the frozen corridors toward the arena, Kira turned to Cefiro. "Can Ivan conjure ice weapons? Like you did in the desert?"</p>
</div>


Nikolai overheard and laughed—a deep, booming sound that echoed off the glacier walls. Anastasia smiled, a rare and quiet expression. Vanya snorted. Nadya giggled. Even Cefiro's mouth twitched with something that was almost amusement. Only Ivan did not laugh. His pale blue eyes dropped to the floor, and his paw tightened around his ceremonial dagger.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Ivan is too young,"</span> Nikolai said, his voice still carrying the warmth of his laughter. <span class="speech">"He has not gone through the ice."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira's face went blank. "Gone through the ice?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"When a Snow Paw turns fifteen, he must travel the tundra alone for a month. No companions. No aid. No weapons but what he can make with his own paws. He must survive the white bears and the ice storms and the cold that freezes the breath in your lungs. And when he returns—"</span> Nikolai extended his massive paw, and a great halberd materialized from the frozen air, its blade gleaming with an inner light, its shaft etched with runic patterns that Kira could not read. <span class="speech">"His weapon is already revealed to him. The ice chooses. We do not."</span>
</div>


As if on cue, the rest of the family conjured their weapons. Anastasia raised one graceful paw, and a shield of crystalline ice formed on her forearm, its surface shimmering with protective runes, while a one-handed sword of pure frost materialized in her other paw. Vanya gripped a great dual-bladed axe, its edges jagged and hungry, cold mist curling from its blade. Cefiro produced his two sabers—one short, one long—their familiar forms gleaming in the blue luminescence.

Nadya flicked her wrists, and a pair of ice daggers appeared between her fingers, their points sharp and eager.


Ivan watched them all with the poorly concealed envy of a cub who had been left out of a game.


<div class="dialogue-block">
Kira stared at the weapons, her dark eyes wide. <span class="speech">"You're like Brother! You can conjure weapons too!"</span> Then her brow furrowed. <span class="speech">"But... he needs his aura. He can't hold them for long. He has to keep writing in the grimoire."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Cefiro scoffed. "He is nothing like us. Our weapons do not need aura. We practice sorcery from cubhood—true sorcery, not the borrowed formulas your brother scribbles in his book. My sabers are imbued with special runes, forged in the ice. I can throw them and make new ones. They are harder than steel and lighter than wood. Your brother cannot let go of his weapons for more than a few seconds or they disappear into motes of green fire. His weapons are borrowed. Ours are made."</p>
</div>


Vanya grinned and swung her axe in a lazy arc. A blade of cutting wind shot across the corridor and passed just above Kira's head, slicing a thin line through the ice wall behind her.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Anastasia's voice cut through the corridor like a whip. "VANYA! NOT IN THE PALACE!"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Vanya's grin did not falter. "I was demonstrating."</p>
</div>


Kira looked at the line carved into the ice wall. She looked at Vanya's axe. She looked at Cefiro's sabers. Then she looked back at Ivan, who was still holding his small ceremonial dagger—the only blade he was permitted to carry, the only blade he could not conjure.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Her expression was perfectly, deliberately blank. "Oh. So Brother is way better. He can change his weapon anytime. He can switch from a saber to a spear to a staff to a sword in the middle of a fight. You're all stuck with one weapon. I get it now."</p>
</div>


Nikolai's laugh boomed through the corridor, even louder than before. Cefiro closed his eyes and let out a long, slow breath. Vanya's grin turned sharp. Ivan looked at Kira with an expression that was somewhere between admiration and despair.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Anastasia's quiet voice carried a note of dry amusement. "The southern princess has a point. The Four Pillars—the Lightbringer's art—is designed to be adaptable. The ice weapons are designed to be enduring. Different philosophies. Neither is better."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Ours are better," <span class="speech-attr">Vanya said.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Ours are better," <span class="speech-attr">Cefiro said at the same time.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira raised her wooden saber. "Ajani's are better. Let's go to the arena. I'll prove it."</p>
</div>


Kira stepped into the arena. The floor was polished ice, enchanted to never melt, its surface gleaming under the pale northern sun. The walls rose in frozen tiers, carved with the history of the Snow Paws—battles against white bears, hunts across the tundra, the long migration north after the Black Fire. The Silverpelt family took their places along the rim. Nikolai settled into a great throne of ice, Anastasia beside him.

Vanya leaned against the wall with her axe resting across her shoulders. Nadya perched on the edge of her seat, her daggers still flickering between her fingers. Cefiro stood apart, his pale blue eyes watching Kira with the quiet assessment of someone who had trained her for six days in the desert and was curious to see what she would do.


Ivan stood across from her, his sparring blade—a practice sword of enchanted ice, blunted but still deadly—held in both paws. His white fur bristled with nervous energy, his tail twitching. He had been trained by his brother. He had watched his sisters spar for years. He had never fought anyone like Kira.


Kira closed her eyes.


'"Most people think the saber is like a tide wolf. Fast. Hard. Destructive." Ajani's voice echoed in her memory, the voice of her brother teaching her the forms in the cold sand of the training arena before dawn. "But I was stuck fighting Iris Serpents for a year. The green deaths. Fifteen meters of iridescent scales and venom and speed. And I'll tell you—the saber is not a wolf. It is like a serpent. Treacherous.

"Annoying. Poisonous. It doesn't overwhelm. It waits. It coils. And when the moment is right—" He had demonstrated the form, his blade flickering out in a strike that Kira's eyes could barely follow. "It bites. This is my fifth form. Serpent's Fang. It's the one that saved me against Sylva. It's the one that will save you."


She opened her eyes.


"You are a Shadow Paw." Lira's voice, quiet and cold, the voice of the assassin who had been assigned to guard her after the second attempt on her life. "The Eight Points are your birthright. But you are small. You are young. You cannot overpower anyone. So you must be smarter. Faster. More ruthless. There is no honor in death. Only in living another day. Remember that."


Kira settled into her stance. The wooden saber was in her right paw. Her left paw was open, empty, ready to trap. The Serpent's Fang—a strike that coiled low and then twisted upward at the last moment, deceptive and venomous. The Eight Points—a philosophy of survival, not glory. She was a Brightmane by adoption, a Shadow Paw by blood. She carried the training of a king and the instincts of an assassin. And she was ready.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ivan raised his ice blade. "Are you prepared, southern princess?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira's mouth twitched. "Are you, northern prince?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai raised one massive paw. "Begin."</p>
</div>


Ivan moved first. He was fast—Cefiro had trained him, and the Snow Paw style was built on precision and economy—but Kira had been trained by a king and an assassin, and she did not meet his charge. She sidestepped, her wooden saber flicking out in a low, coiling strike that Ivan barely parried. The ice blade rang against the wood, and Kira was already moving again, her left paw open, her body flowing around his counter like water around stone.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"You're quick,"</span> Ivan said, his voice tight with concentration. <span class="speech">"Cefiro taught you."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Cefiro taught me to correct my footwork. Ajani taught me to fight."</span> She feinted low, and when Ivan dropped his guard to block, her saber twisted upward at the last moment—the Serpent's Fang, coiling and treacherous—and the wooden blade tapped him gently on the shoulder. <span class="speech">"Dead."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ivan stared at the saber. "That was—what was that?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"The Serpent's Fang. Ajani's fifth form. He used it to defeat Sylva of the Motted Paws in the tournament. She was the best fighter in the south. He beat her with this."</span> She stepped back. <span class="speech">"Again."</span>
</div>


They clashed again. Ivan was learning—he adapted quickly, his parries sharper, his counters faster—but Kira was relentless. She fought like someone who had been told the saber was the survivor's weapon, and she was determined to survive. She used the Eight Points—the elbows, the knees, the low kicks that Lira had drilled into her in the quiet hours of the night. She used the ice blade techniques that Cefiro had taught her in the desert.

She used everything she had learned from everyone who had ever believed in her.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ivan's blade caught her shoulder—a glancing blow, but enough to make her hiss. "Dead," he said, his voice breathless.</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Barely. You'd be dead three times over if this was real."</span> She lunged, and her saber caught him in the ribs. <span class="speech">"Dead again."</span> She was grinning now, her dark fur damp with sweat, her eyes blazing. <span class="speech">"You're good. Cefiro taught you well."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"You're better."</span> Ivan lowered his ice blade, his chest heaving. <span class="speech">"I thought—I thought the southern Wengari were soft. I thought they had forgotten how to fight. Cefiro said the Bright Paws were humiliated in the tournament. I thought all of you were like that."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"The Bright Paws were soft. Ajani fixed them. He fixed all of us."</span> She lowered her saber. <span class="speech">"You're not soft. You're just young. When you go through the ice, you'll be stronger. And then we'll spar again."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ivan looked at her for a long moment. Then, very quietly, he said, "I would like that."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Above them, Nikolai leaned toward Anastasia. "She fights like a cornered snow fox. She will be a terror when she is grown."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Anastasia's quiet voice was thoughtful. "She already is. And she has the training of a king and an assassin. Ivan will have to work very hard to keep up with her."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai smiled. "Good. He has been coddled too long. A difficult wife will be good for his character."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"They are six years old, Nikolai."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"In Wengari years, perhaps. In Snow Paw years, they are nearly adolescents. And in political years—"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I know. You said that already." <span class="speech-attr">But she did not contradict him. She simply watched the two young Wengari standing in the arena, their weapons lowered, their breathing still heavy, and she began to plan.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Later at night Nikolai ask Cefiro "so, the Truth son, what do you make of the South and it's king?"</p>
</div>


The night had settled over the Ice City like a blanket of frozen stars. From the observation spire, the northern lights rippled across the sky in curtains of green and gold and violet, their reflections dancing on the glacier walls. Nikolai stood at the edge of the platform, his massive paws resting on the frozen railing, his pale blue eyes fixed on the distant horizon where the tundra stretched into endless darkness. Cefiro stood beside him, his white fur blending with the snow-dusted stone.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"So, the Truth."</span> Nikolai's voice was quiet, stripped of the booming authority he wore in the dining hall. <span class="speech">"What do you make of the South? And its king?"</span>
</div>


Cefiro was silent for a long moment. The northern lights flickered overhead, and when he spoke, his voice was careful and measured, the voice of a prince giving his father the assessment he had asked for.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The South is not what we imagined. For five thousand years, we told ourselves we were the last Wengari. The ones who fled. The ones who survived. We told ourselves the others had perished in the Black Fire, or in the wars that followed. We were wrong. They are not dead. They are not soft. They are not weak. They are not what we imagined at all."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">He paused. "There are four families now. Five, if you count us. The Bright Paws rule from the desert capital. They were soft—the tournament exposed them. Ajani has been rebuilding them. The Stripe Paws are merchants and mercenaries. They control the trade routes and the caravans. They are the economic spine of the Wengari. The Shadow Paws are assassins and spies. They carry the shame of the First Tyrant, but Ajani has given them something they have not had in five thousand years: legitimacy. The Motted Paws are silent observers. They have been patient for millennia, waiting for a king worth following. They have found one."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"And the king?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Cefiro turned to face his father. "Ajani Brightmane. I sparred with him three years ago. He was a starving wanderer who could barely hold a blade. He was stubborn. He kept getting up. I thought he was foolish. I was wrong. He is a White Dawn. Convergence-born. Touched by both suns. He renewed the pact with the lord of the desert. He trained under the lord of the marsh. He freed the Pyrinae, made allies of the Veylar, opened trade with the Threx. He held a tournament and put his crown on the line. He survived an assassination attempt, a coup, a coma, and a war. He summoned six elemental spirits and destroyed a Plague creature with a thunderstorm. He has done more in one year than most kings do in a lifetime. He is the most powerful Wengari I have ever seen. He is also the most oblivious. He did not notice when two women tried to court him. He did not notice when an assassin tried to kill him until his scorpion melted the man. He laughs when his scorpions frighten the servants. He wears a crooked belt his sister made him and refuses to take it off. He is kind and terrifying and reckless and brilliant and utterly, infuriatingly unaware of his own legend."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai was silent for a long moment. The northern lights rippled across his white fur. "You like him."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I respect him. I fear him. I am proud to call him cousin. I do not know if I could fight him now. I do not know if anyone could. And I do not know if he will survive what is coming."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The endless darkness. The thing in the belt. You mentioned it in your report."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The lord of the desert spoke of it. The lord of the marsh spoke of it. The Dragari crossed the desert to warn him. The creature that rose in the Plague War—the shadowy figure that gave the Humman Sultan his weapons—was merely a servant. The true enemy is still out there. It has been there since before the Wengari walked the desert. It has destroyed civilizations. It has been waiting. And Ajani is building a coalition to fight it. He does not yet know this. He thinks he is building a road. He is building an army."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai turned to face his son fully. "And the Snow Paws? What role does he want us to play in this?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"He wants us to come home. He sent the royal seal and an invitation. He wants formal diplomatic relations. He wants trade. He wants the fifth family to return. But more than that—"</span> Cefiro paused. <span class="speech">"He wants us to fight. When the darkness comes, he will need every Wengari who can hold a blade. He does not know he is asking this. He has not asked it yet. But he will. And we must decide whether we will answer."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Nikolai looked out at the northern lights for a long, quiet moment. When he spoke, his voice was very soft. <span class="speech">"Five thousand years. We have been alone for five thousand years. And now a White Dawn rises in the south, and the ancient enemy stirs in the belt, and our long-lost cousins are fighting a war we did not know existed. I will go south. I will meet this king. I will spar with him. And then—"</span> He placed one massive paw on his son's shoulder. <span class="speech">"Then we will decide. The Snow Paws have been running from the darkness for five thousand years. Perhaps it is time we turned and faced it."</span>
</div>


The northern lights flickered overhead, and far to the south, across the endless desert and the blackened sand, Ajani Brightmane sat on his throne with two scorpions curled at his feet and a crooked belt around his waist, and he did not yet know that the fifth family was coming home.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">The next day at breakfast, Nikolai set down his cup of Frostfire and made his announcement. "We go to meet our cousins in the south. Tell the servants to pack for a six-month voyage."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira's eyes lit up. "Six months? It took us five days—if you don't count the time we were lost."</p>
</div>


The breakfast table fell silent. Kira's words hung in the air like a thrown snowball, and every member of the Silverpelt family turned to look at Cefiro with expressions ranging from confusion to dawning amusement.


<div class="dialogue-block">
Nikolai's massive paw froze halfway to his cup of Frostfire. <span class="speech">"Five days,"</span> he repeated slowly. <span class="speech">"Not counting the time you were lost. How much time were you lost?"</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira answered before Cefiro could speak. "Two days. He was navigating by the wrong stars. He said the constellations in the south are different. He didn't want to tell me because he didn't want me to worry. I yelled at him."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"I recall the yelling,"</span> Cefiro said quietly. <span class="speech">"It was very loud for someone so small."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"You deserved it. You said you knew the way."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I did know the way. I merely took a more scenic route than intended."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Vanya was grinning openly now. "You were lost. In the desert. For two days. And you didn't tell the cub because you were too proud."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I was not too proud. I was being considerate of her emotional state."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"You were lost,"</span> Nadya said, her voice bright with delight. <span class="speech">"The great Cefiro, the best fighter in the Ice City, got lost in the desert because he was looking at the wrong stars."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I was not looking at the wrong stars. The stars were wrong. It is a legitimate astronomical distinction."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai set down his cup of Frostfire. The liquid inside was still swirling from the force of his son's choking fit. "You told me the journey took you six months. You told me you nearly died. You told me the desert was impassable."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Cefiro's pale blue eyes were fixed on the table. <span class="speech">"The journey south did take six months. I was alone. I did not know the way. The Dragari guided me part of the distance, but I did not have a destination—I only knew the capital was in the center of the desert. I wandered. The Sunraptor nearly died. I nearly died. When I arrived, I charted the route. The journey back took five days because I knew where I was going. And because—"</span> He paused. <span class="speech">"Because Kira navigated. She is very good at navigating. She navigated the market for three years without getting caught. She is—"</span> He stopped again, and the words seemed to pain him. <span class="speech">"She is a better navigator than I am."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira's dark eyes widened. "Did you just admit I'm better than you at something?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I admitted nothing. I stated a fact. Navigation is not my primary skill. I am very good at ice blades. And Frostfire. And—"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"You got us lost,"</span> Kira said. <span class="speech">"For two days. Because you were looking at the wrong stars."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The stars were different."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"That's what you said. For two days. While we flew in circles."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Anastasia's quiet, frightening stillness had taken on a quality that was almost—almost—maternal. "The southern princess navigated for you. Through the desert. At night. Using stars she had never seen before."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"She said the constellations looked like animals. She named one of them 'the scorpion' because it had a tail that curved. I did not see the tail. She was correct."</p>
</div>


Pearl clicked her pincers from her place on Kira's shoulder. The sound managed to convey smugness.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai looked at his son for a long, silent moment. Then he looked at Kira. Then he looked back at his son. "You were lost in the desert for two days. The cub navigated for you. And you did not tell me any of this when I announced a six-month voyage."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I was going to mention it. Eventually. When the Frostfire was less strong."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Nikolai let out a long, slow breath. <span class="speech">"The servants are packing for six months. The entire delegation. The white bears. The trade goods. The wedding—"</span> He stopped. <span class="speech">"The whatever we were bringing. And you are telling me it takes five days."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Five days if you do not get lost,"</span> Kira said helpfully. <span class="speech">"He got lost. I didn't."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Thank you, cousin. Your support is invaluable."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Nikolai raised one massive paw and rubbed his temples. <span class="speech">"The servants will unpack the six-month provisions. We will repack for a two-week journey. Cefiro, you will lead the delegation. Kira, you will navigate."</span> He looked at his son with the weary expression of a father who had long ago accepted that his children would humble him in ways he could never anticipate. <span class="speech">"And for the love of the ice, do not get lost again."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I will not get lost. Kira is navigating."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Then we are all doomed,"</span> Vanya said cheerfully. <span class="speech">"The southern princess is six years old and has been to the Ice City once. We will end up in the Flickermarch."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"I got us here,"</span> Kira said, her voice sharp. <span class="speech">"I can get us back."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"And if you fail, we will wander the desert for six months and eat our own supplies and die of thirst."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Vanya,"</span> Anastasia said quietly, <span class="speech">"enough."</span>
</div>


Vanya subsided, still grinning. Cefiro was staring very intently at his cup of Frostfire, as if hoping it might swallow him whole. Nikolai looked at the assembled family—his terrifying wife, his warrior daughter, his fierce youngest daughter, his lost and found eldest son, his smitten youngest son, and the dark-furred southern princess who had navigated across the desert using stars she had named after scorpions.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"We leave in three days,"</span> he said. <span class="speech">"Pack lightly. Bring gifts. And someone please tell the servants about the white bears. They are not to be ridden. They are gifts. I do not want a repeat of the Sunraptor incident."</span> He rose from the table, his massive form casting a long shadow across the frozen dining hall. <span class="speech">"The Snow Paws are going south. After five thousand years, we are going home."</span>
</div>


The morning of the departure dawned cold and clear. The great gates of the Ice City stood open, and the delegation was assembled in the courtyard. Kira stood beside Cefiro, Pearl curled against her chest, her dark eyes scanning the courtyard for whatever beasts of burden the Snow Paws would ride. She saw the white bears first—massive creatures with thick fur and patient eyes. But they were not the ones being loaded. They were gifts, the servants had said. Not mounts.


Then she saw them. Emerging from vast tunnels carved into the glacier, their chitin gleaming with the pale blue luminescence of the Ice City's enchantments. They were enormous—each one longer than three Wohs placed end to end—their bodies segmented like the millipedes she had seen in the gardens of Styxian, but far larger. Far faster, she suspected. Their many legs moved in rippling waves, and as they crawled, the ground beneath them froze solid, a thin sheet of ice spreading before them like a path.



<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"What are those?" <span class="speech-attr">Kira breathed.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai turned. "Snow wurms. They are the reason the Ice City has survived for five thousand years. Flicker-attuned arthropods. Short-lived—five or six years at most—but they breed quickly and they are very, very fast."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira watched one of the wurms glide across the courtyard, its hundreds of legs skimming over the ice it had created. It moved with an eerie, fluid grace, its body undulating in waves, and it was carrying an entire contingent of Snow Paw warriors and their supplies on its back as if they weighed nothing. "They freeze the ground beneath them with their aura. Then they glide over it. Two wurms can carry forty Wengari with provisions. They can carry other beasts of burden as well. The white bears will ride on the second wurm."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira's eyes were very wide. "How fast are they?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Very fast. We will cross the tundra in a day. The desert in perhaps three, if the weather holds and the wurms do not tire."</span> He paused. <span class="speech">"They will tire. The desert is not their natural terrain. But we have brought extra wurms to rotate. They will manage. Five days, as you said. Six, if we are unlucky."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ivan appeared at Kira's side. "They're beautiful, aren't they? When I was small, I used to sneak into the wurm tunnels to watch them molt. Father was very angry. They are not dangerous, but they are fragile when they molt. You must be very quiet."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira looked at the wurms. Then at Ivan. Then back at the wurms. "Can I ride at the front? I want to see the ice path."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Ivan's face lit up. <span class="speech">"I will ask Father. He will say yes. He always says yes when I ask about the wurms."</span> He paused. <span class="speech">"Also, he wants you to like it here. He wants you to come back."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"He wants me to marry you."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ivan's face went very pale beneath his white fur. "He—he said that?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"He didn't say it. He just thinks it very loudly. And your mother thinks it too. And Vanya. And Nadya. And Cefiro. Everyone thinks it. You're the only one who doesn't know everyone is watching."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Ivan stared at her. <span class="speech">"Are you—do you—is that—"</span> He could not complete a sentence. Kira patted his arm with the same brisk, practical gesture she used when calming Pearl. <span class="speech">"You're very nice. You're brave. You didn't laugh when I said I wanted to fight. But I'm six. You're six. We're going to meet Ajani. We're going to see the south. Ask me again in ten years."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Ten years,"</span> Ivan repeated. He seemed to be calculating. <span class="speech">"That is a long time."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I'm worth waiting for." <span class="speech-attr">She turned and walked toward the lead snow wurm, Pearl's antennae waving in approval. Ivan stared after her. His tail twitched. He was smiling.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Vanya appeared at his side. "She's terrifying."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"She's magnificent."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"She's six."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"She said to ask her again in ten years."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Vanya blinked. <span class="speech">"That's not a rejection. That's a promise."</span> She looked at her youngest brother with something that was almost respect. <span class="speech">"You're less stupid than you look, Ivan."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The whole family thinks I'm stupid."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
