<p class="speech-line">Nikolai looked down at the Sultan. The massive Tsar did not speak for a long moment. Then he tilted his head, his pale blue eyes taking in the two honor guards, the cargo birds, the small, humble delegation that had crossed the desert to beg for peace. "You are the one who replaced the fool who started the war," he said. It was not a question.</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Salahim straightened. "I am. Mekhmed was a rogue king. He did not act with the consent of the Council of Merchants. I have come to make amends. I have brought—"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"We know what you have brought."</span> Vanya's voice was sharp, her axe still resting across her shoulder. <span class="speech">"We saw the cargo birds from the wall. You are very brave or very stupid to come here with only two guards."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"The Sultan is not here to fight,"</span> Nadya added, her ice daggers still flickering between her fingers. <span class="speech">"He's here to grovel. Let him grovel. It's been a very boring morning."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
Nikolai let out a low, rumbling chuckle. <span class="speech">"My daughters. One wants to fight you. One wants to watch you beg. I am inclined to let neither happen until the king has seen you."</span> He lowered his halberd, the runic patterns along its blade dimming slightly. <span class="speech">"You may enter the city, Sultan Salahim. The White Dawn is in the atrium. He is in a good mood. He has been handing out titles all morning. If you are wise, you will wait until he asks for your reparations before you offer them. He does not like being interrupted."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Salahim inclined his head. "I am grateful for the advice, Tsar..."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Nikolai Silverpelt. Tsar of the Snow Paws. The fifth family. We have been gone for five thousand years. We came back."</span> He smiled, and it was not entirely reassuring. <span class="speech">"You will find the Wengari are full of surprises."</span>
</div>


Vanya and Nadya fell into step behind their father, and the three Snow Paws escorted the Humman delegation through the northern gate. The cargo birds followed, their green plumage ruffling in the desert wind, their pilots glancing nervously at the massive white Wengari who had appeared out of nowhere like a ghost from the frozen north. The Sultan walked with his back straight and his face composed, but his heart was pounding.

He had expected hostility. He had not expected to be greeted by a family of giants who had been missing for five thousand years and seemed to find the entire situation mildly amusing. This was going to be a very long afternoon.


Nikolai let out a great, billowing roar and swung his halberd in a wide, lazy arc—the gesture of a warrior who had been waiting for something to do and was finally, gloriously, about to do it. The Humman honor guards flinched. The cargo birds screeched. Salahim closed his eyes and prepared for death.


Then Nikolai stopped.


His nose twitched. The halberd paused mid-swing, its runic patterns still blazing. The Tsar of the Snow Paws, champion of the frozen wastes, defender of the Great Ice City, sniffed the air once. Twice. His pale blue eyes narrowed. All the fighting spirit drained from his massive frame, replaced by something that looked very much like disbelief.


Vanya smelled it next. Her axe lowered. Her sharp, warrior's expression twisted into something halfway between disgust and incredulity. Nadya's ice daggers stopped flickering. She pressed one paw to her nose and made a small, strangled sound.


Salahim felt the warmth spreading through his own robes. His eyes were still closed. He did not dare look back at his honor guards. He did not dare look at the cargo birds. He did not dare look at anything.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai's voice, when it came, was very quiet. "Did he just..."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">At the wall, M'rak lowered his spyglass. His burn-scarred face was unreadable. "I think he did."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Reva, standing beside him, squinted at the distant scene. "One of the honor guards. The younger one. His knees buckled when the Tsar roared. I believe the fear was... overwhelming."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Zephyr's scarred face twitched. "He soiled himself. In front of the Sultan. In front of the Tsar. In front of the entire Wengari military and every delegation on the continent."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Yvaria's drum harness creaked as she leaned forward. "Is it wrong that I feel sorry for him?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Yes," <span class="speech-attr">said M'rak, Reva, and Zephyr in unison.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"No,"</span> said Kira, who had somehow appeared beside them with Pearl on her shoulder and Ivan trailing behind her. <span class="speech">"It's not wrong. He's probably very embarrassed. Also, what's that smell?"</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"It's coming from the gate,"</span> Ivan said helpfully. <span class="speech">"I think one of the Hummans had an accident."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"An accident,"</span> Kira repeated. <span class="speech">"In his pants. In front of everyone."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"That is what an accident means, yes."</p>
</div>


Pearl clicked her pincers once. Remembered.


<div class="dialogue-block">
At the gate, Nikolai lowered his halberd entirely. His fighting spirit was gone. His desire to intimidate the Humman delegation had evaporated. He looked at the young honor guard, who was trembling so violently that his armor was rattling, and then he looked at Salahim, who had still not opened his eyes. <span class="speech">"Your man has soiled himself,"</span> Nikolai said. <span class="speech">"I do not know the protocol for this. In the Ice City, we would offer him a change of clothes and never speak of it again. What do the Hummans do?"</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Salahim opened his eyes. His face was very pale. "We also never speak of it again. Ideally. Ever."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"I think the cargo birds are laughing,"</span> Nadya said. <span class="speech">"Is that possible? Can cargo birds laugh?"</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Cargo birds cannot laugh,"</span> Vanya said. <span class="speech">"But those cargo birds are definitely laughing."</span>
</div>





Nikolai turned. The great halberd dissolved into a swirl of snowflakes, and the Tsar of the Snow Paws, champion of the frozen wastes, defender of the Great Ice City, began the long, slow walk back to the gate. His massive shoulders were slumped. His pale blue eyes were fixed on the distant golden walls of Styxian with the hollow stare of a warrior who had prepared for battle and been defeated by something he could not fight.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Come, Humman. And perhaps next time, bring better guards."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Salahim fell into step behind the Tsar, his two honor guards following in silence. The young guard who had soiled himself was walking very carefully, his face a deep, mortified crimson, his eyes fixed on the sand. The older guard was staring straight ahead with the expression of a man who had decided to forget this day had ever happened. Vanya and Nadya followed behind them, their weapons dismissed, their earlier excitement replaced by the quiet, glum disappointment of warriors who had been denied a fight. Nadya looked at the young guard's back with something that was almost pity. "He's going to remember this for the rest of his life."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"He's lucky he has a rest of his life,"</span> Vanya muttered. <span class="speech">"Father almost killed him."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Father almost killed all of them. Now he's escorting them to the king like a caravan master with a wounded fire foot."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The Fire Foot is not wounded. The Fire Foot is fine. The Fire Foot will be fine for the rest of the journey."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nadya looked at the young guard's trembling shoulders. "I think the Fire Foot might need a change of robes before he meets the White Dawn."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Vanya snorted. It was not a dignified sound. It was the sound of a warrior who had been raised in the frozen wastes, who had fought white bears and ice storms, who had crossed the desert to meet her long-lost cousins, and who had just watched an enemy delegation be completely undone by a single terrified soldier. "This is the worst war party I have ever been part of."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"The only war party you have ever been part of."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"That does not make my statement less true."</p>
</div>


At the gate, the assembled Wengari military was still standing at attention. The four Heavenly Generals—Wind, Earth, Fire, and Shadow—watched the approaching delegation with expressions that ranged from bemusement to carefully concealed mirth. M'rak's burn-scarred face was stone. Reva's golden eyes were bright with barely suppressed laughter. Yvaria was composing a new drum rhythm that sounded suspiciously like a funeral march.

Zephyr's scarred face had not changed, but his shadow wolves were making a low, huffing sound that was unmistakably laughter.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Kira was standing on her throne for a better view. "Is he going to be okay? The one who had the accident?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ivan, standing beside her, considered the question with the gravity of a prince who had been raised in a frozen palace and had never seen a man soil himself in fear before. "I think so. He is walking. He is very red. But he is walking."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"It's going to take him a very long time to live this down," <span class="speech-attr">Kira said.</span></p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"What does 'live this down' mean?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"It means everyone is going to remember this forever and he's going to be embarrassed for the rest of his life."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ivan absorbed this. "That seems unfair. He was very brave to come here at all. The Hummans were our enemies. He probably thought he was going to die."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"He did die,"</span> Kira said. <span class="speech">"Just not the way he expected."</span>
</div>


Pearl clicked her pincers once. Remembered. The scorpion would remember this forever. Everyone would remember this forever. Nikolai reached the gate, his massive frame still carrying the weight of his defeat, and he looked up at Ajani with the expression of a warrior who had been sent to intimidate an enemy delegation and had instead been forced to escort a terrified young man who had soiled himself in front of the entire world.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"They are here,"</span> Nikolai said. <span class="speech">"The Sultan. The honor guards. The cargo birds. The reparations. Everything. I am going to go sit in the shade now. I do not wish to discuss what happened."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ajani looked down at his uncle. The diadem's gem pulsed once. The white spear hummed in his paw. And the White Dawn, who had summoned six elemental spirits and killed a Plague creature with a thunderstorm, who had united the four families and welcomed the fifth, who had just named four Heavenly Generals and the first Knight of the Wengari, looked at the defeated Tsar of the Snow Paws and said the only thing that seemed appropriate. "Did he at least bring clean robes?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai let out a long, slow breath. "I did not ask. I was too busy watching my dignity die."</p>
</div>


The soiled guard would never be permitted to walk through the city in that state—not by the Wengari, who are a warrior race with strict codes of dignity, and not by the Hummans, who would be mortified to present such a figure before the White Dawn. He would have been quietly left behind at the cargo birds to clean himself up. The remaining honor guard would walk with the Sultan, and the young guard's absence would be conspicuous but unmentioned.


Kira and Ivan had come to the wall to watch the commotion. Nikolai, spotting them among the crowd, would have scooped them up—one under each massive arm—and carried them back toward the atrium with the same casual strength he had used to leap from the snow wurm. They would have arrived at the gate with the rest of the procession, Kira still perched on Nikolai's shoulder, Ivan tucked under his other arm, both of them thoroughly unbothered by the mode of transport.


The four Heavenly Generals had been at the wall as well, watching the cargo birds descend. They walked back in formation behind the Snow Paws, their ceremonial armor gleaming, their expressions carefully composed. The rest of the military fell into step behind them, a slow, dignified procession escorting the Humman delegation to the throne.


The walk back to the atrium was long and very quiet. Nikolai led the way, his massive frame slumping more with each step, Kira still on his shoulder, Ivan still under his arm. Vanya and Nadya walked behind him, their weapons dismissed, their earlier battle-lust replaced by the glum silence of warriors who had been promised a fight and been denied. Salahim walked behind them, his one remaining honor guard at his side, the young guard's absence a silent, mortifying reminder of how the encounter had actually gone.

The cargo birds had been left at the gate, their pilots instructed to wait.


Ajani watched the procession approach from the throne, the diadem's gem pulsing with a slow, steady light. He had seen the war horn sound. He had seen the Snow Paws vanish. He had seen the long, slow walk back. He had noticed the missing guard. He had noticed Nikolai's defeated posture. He had noticed the Sultan's pale face. And when the Tsar reached the atrium and set Kira and Ivan gently on the ground, Ajani leaned forward on the throne and spoke with the careful, measured voice of a king who was trying very hard not to laugh.



<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"Uncle. You were gone for quite some time. Did the Hummans give you trouble?"</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai looked up at his nephew. His pale blue eyes were hollow. His massive shoulders were slumped. His voice, when he found it, was very quiet. "The Hummans did not give me trouble. One of the honor guards had an accident. I do not wish to discuss it further. Please receive the Sultan so I may go sit in the shade and forget this day ever happened."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ajani looks at Nikolai confused then to sulheim with a questioning look, sulheim says "I have utterly forgotten what happened, may I explain why I am here ? If it's not too much trouble I would also like to sit on the shade"</p>
</div>


Ajani looked from Nikolai's defeated posture to the Sultan's carefully composed face. The Sultan was weary—not merely from the desert journey, but from the ordeal of the past few minutes, from the war, from the weight of a crown he had not wanted and a throne he had inherited from a fool. The diadem's gem pulsed once, a slow, steady beat. Then the White Dawn did something that surprised everyone present.


He laughed. It was not the cold laugh of the throne room, nor the fierce laugh of the arena. It was the genuine, surprised laughter of a young king who had been handed a situation so absurd that there was nothing left to do but find it funny. He gestured toward a shaded alcove near the edge of the atrium, where the Pyrinae had woven a canopy of rune-glass and living vines to shield the Veylar queen from the desert heat.

The queen had declined to attend, and her seat remained empty, a quiet pool of cool shade in the blazing morning.


<div class="dialogue-block">
<span class="speech">"Please, Sultan. Sit. It seems my uncle has already given you enough of a welcome."</span> He paused, and the faintest twitch of his whiskers betrayed him. <span class="speech">"Whatever it was that happened, I am certain neither of us wishes to discuss it. Let us agree that nothing of note occurred at the gate, and move on to the matter of your reparations."</span>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Salahim inclined his head, and the relief that washed across his weathered face was visible to everyone. "Nothing of note occurred at the gate. I have utterly forgotten it. May I explain why I am here? If it's not too much trouble, I would also like to sit in the shade. The desert is very hot. I am not as young as I once was."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Ajani gestured again toward the shaded alcove. "Please. My uncle was just going to sit in the shade himself. You may share the canopy. Consider it a gesture of goodwill between our peoples."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai, already trudging toward the alcove, paused and looked back over his shoulder. "You are making me share the shade with the Humman."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">"I am making you share the shade with the Sultan of the Hummans, who has come to offer reparations and who has very graciously agreed to forget whatever happened at the gate. You will sit together. You will be civil. You will drink the cold tea the Pyrinae have prepared. This is my command as your king and your nephew."</p>
</div>


<div class="dialogue-block">
<p class="speech-line">Nikolai stared at Ajani for a long moment. Then he let out a low, rumbling sigh. "Fine. But I am taking the larger cushion."</p>
</div>
